# Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim

ברטנורא על משנה נדרים

Link: [https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim)

חיבור התשתית הקדום של חז״ל, המתעד מגוון דעות הלכתיות ממסורת התורה שבעל פה. נחתם בשנת 200 לספירה לערך.

16th-century commentary printed in most editions of the Mishnah, including summaries of talmudic discussions and legal conclusions.

Sections: Chapter-Mishnah-Comment

Location: Mishnah/Rishonim on Mishnah/Bartenura/Seder Nashim

## Chapter 1


### Mishnah 1

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:1:1)


**כָּל כִּנּוּיֵי נְדָרִים כִּנְדָרִים.** בַּגְּמָרָא מוֹקְמִינַן דְּרֵישָׁא דְּמַתְנִיתִין חַסּוֹרֵי מִחַסְּרָא וְהָכִי קָתָנֵי, כָּל יְדוֹת נְדָרִים כִּנְדָרִים, כָּל כִּנּוּיֵי נְדָרִים כִּנְדָרִים. אֵלּוּ הֵן יְדוֹת נְדָרִים, הָאוֹמֵר לַחֲבֵרוֹ מֻדָּר אֲנִי מִמְּךָ מֻפְרָשׁ אֲנִי מִמְּךָ וְכוּ'. אֵלּוּ הֵן כִּנּוּיֵי נְדָרִים, קוֹנָם, קוֹנָח, קוֹנָס וְכוּ'. יְדוֹת נְדָרִים, כְּמוֹ בֵּית יָד שֶׁל כְּלִי שֶׁאוֹחֲזִין אוֹתוֹ בּוֹ, כָּךְ יְדוֹת נְדָרִים שֶׁבָּהֶם הַנְּדָרִים נֶאֱחָזִים. כִּנּוּיֵי נְדָרִים, כְּמוֹ הַמְכַנֶּה שֵׁם לַחֲבֵרוֹ [בָּבָא מְצִיעָא נח:] שֶׁאֵינוֹ עִקָּרוֹ שֶׁל שֵׁם:


In the Gemara (Tractate Nedarim 2b), we maintain that our Mishnah is deficient and should be read as follows: all intimations/ידות (i.e., a term for words uttered by a person conveying a certain intention that can be understood from the context or from the general subject of the statement, although it is not clearly and explicitly expressed – are intimations regarded as explicit statements) of vows are like vows; all substitutes/כנויי for the language of vows (i.e., and it is obvious that he intended his statement to be a vow, his words assume that status) are like vows. Which are intimations of vows? A person who says to his fellow: “I am forbidden by vow from you,” “I am separated from you,” etc. Which are substitutions of vows? “A person who states (see Mishnah 2 of this chapter): קונם/Konam, קונח/Konakh, קונס/Konas, etc., (i.e., a specific type of vow whereby one prohibits himself from eating something or deriving benefit from something or someone by saying: “That person or object is to a KONAM;” the word KONAM is a substitute for the Hebrew word for offering/קרבן – and is used in order to avoid uttering that word). The intimations of vows/ידות נדרים are like the handle of a utensil that one holds it by, so are intimations of vows by which vows are held. Substitutes for vows, like [Tractate Bava Metzia 58b] when one calls his neighbor by a nickname which is not the essence of the name [of that person].


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:1:2)


**מֻדָּרְנִי מִמְּךָ.** אִם אָמַר אֶחָד מִלְּשׁוֹנוֹת הַלָּלוּ, מֻדָּר אֲנִי מִמְּךָ שֶׁאֵינִי אוֹכֵל לְךָ וְשֶׁאֵינִי טוֹעֵם לְךָ, אוֹ מֻפְרָשׁ אֲנִי מִמְּךָ שֶׁאֵינִי אוֹכֵל לְךָ וְשֶׁאֵינִי טוֹעֵם לְךָ, אוֹ מְרֻחָק אֲנִי מִמְּךָ שֶׁאֵינִי אוֹכֵל לְךָ וְאֵינִי טוֹעֵם לְךָ, הוּא יְדוֹת נְדָרִים וְאָסוּר לֶאֱכֹל וְלִטְעֹם עִמּוֹ. אֲבָל אִם אָמַר לוֹ מֻדָּר אֲנִי מִמְּךָ בִּלְבַד, אֵין בְּמַשְׁמָעוּת דְּבָרָיו אֶלָּא שֶׁלֹּא יְדַבֵּר עִמּוֹ. וּמֻפְרָשׁ אֲנִי מִמְּךָ בִּלְבַד, מַשְׁמַע שֶׁלֹּא יִשָּׂא וְיִתֵּן עִמּוֹ. וּמְרֻחָק אֲנִי מִמְּךָ בִּלְבַד, מַשְׁמַע שֶׁלֹּא יֵשֵׁב בְּאַרְבַּע אַמּוֹתָיו. וְאֵינוֹ אָסוּר לֶאֱכֹל עִמּוֹ אֶלָּא אִם כֵּן פֵּרֵשׁ וְאָמַר עִם כָּל אֶחָד מִלְּשׁוֹנוֹת הַלָּלוּ, שֶׁאֵינִי אוֹכֵל לְךָ וְשֶׁאֵינִי טוֹעֵם לְךָ:


מודר אני ממך – if he said one of these linguistic formulations: “I am forbidden by vow from you that I don’t eat your [food], or/and I taste your [food],” or “I am separated from you that I don’t eat your [food], or/and if I taste your [food],” or “I am distanced from you that I don’t eat your [food] or/and I don’t taste your [food],” this is an intimation of vows and it is forbidden to eat or to taste with him. But if he said to him: “I am forbidden by vow” alone, there is no implication in his words other than he doesn’t speak with him. And “I am separated from you” alone, implies that he will not engage in business with him, and “I am distanced from you” alone, implies that he will not sit within his four cubits, and does not prohibit eating with him unless he specified and stated with one of these linguistic formulations, “that I don’t eat [food] with you,” or “I don’t taste anything with you.”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:1:3)


**רַבִּי עֲקִיבָא הָיָה חוֹכֵךְ וְכוּ'.** כְּלוֹמַר מְחַכֵּךְ שְׂפָתָיו זוֹ בָזוֹ וְלֹא רָצָה לֶאֱסֹר בְּפֵרוּשׁ, אֲבָל הָיָה נִרְאֶה מִדַּעְתּוֹ שֶׁהָיָה אוֹסֵר:


רבי עקיבא היה חוכך וכו' (had some hesitation about deciding in favor of greater stringency) – meaning to say, rubbed/scratched his lips on against the other, but did not want to forbid explicitly, but it had appeared from his temperament/mind that it was forbidden.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:1:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:1:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:1:4)


**כְּנִדְרֵי רְשָׁעִים נָדַר בְּנָזִיר וּבְקָרְבָּן וּבִשְׁבוּעָה.** אִם אָמַר הֲרֵי עָלַי כְּנִדְרֵי רְשָׁעִים, שֶׁנִּדְרֵיהֶם נָזִיר וְקָרְבָּן [וּ]שְׁבוּעָה, אִם אֹכַל כִּכָּר זוֹ, וְעָבַר וַאֲכָלָהּ, חַיָּב לִהְיוֹת נָזִיר שְׁלֹשִׁים יוֹם וּלְהָבִיא קָרְבַּן עוֹלָה וְחַיָּב מַלְקוֹת כְּעוֹבֵר עַל שְׁבוּעַת בִּטּוּי, שֶׁהֲרֵי הִזְכִּיר בְּנִדְרוֹ נָזִיר וְקָרְבָּן וּשְׁבוּעָה. וּמַה שֶּׁאָמַר כְּנִדְרֵי רְשָׁעִים, לְפִי שֶׁהָרְשָׁעִים הֵם שֶׁנּוֹדְרִים וְנִשְׁבָּעִים, לֹא הַכְּשֵׁרִים, שֶׁהַכְּשֵׁרִים יְרֵאִים שֶׁלֹּא לַעֲבֹר עַל בַּל תְּאַחֵר וְנִזְהָרִים שֶׁלֹּא לְהוֹצִיא שְׁבוּעָה מִפִּיהֶם, וּלְפִיכָךְ כְּנִדְרֵי כְּשֵׁרִים לֹא אָמַר כְּלוּם:


כנדרי רשעים נדר בנזיר ובקרבן ובשבועה – if he said: “May this be upon me like the vows of wicked people whose vows in the case of a Nazir or in bringing a sacrifice or in taking of an oath if I eat this loaf, and he transgressed and ate it, he is liable to become a Nazir for thity days and to bring a sacrifice of a burnt offering and would liable for flogging/stripes like one who transgresses an oath on a statement (i.e., an oath taken by a person to reinforce a promise or an obligation or o confirm he veracity of a story –he brings a sacrifice based upon his financial situation/קרבן עולה ויורד ) because he mentioned in his oath the Nazir, and/or a sacrifice and/or an oath. But when he stated, “like the vows of the wicked,” since he wicked are those who make vows and take oaths, not the suitable people, for he suitable ones fear not to transgress (Deuteronomy 23:22): “[When you make a vow to the LORD your God,] do not put off fulfilling it, [for the LORD your God will require it of you, and you will have incurred guilt],” and they (i.e., the suitable/appropriate people) are warned not to bring forth an oath from their lips, and therefore “like the vows of the suitable/appropriate [people],” he has said nothing whatsoever.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:1:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:1:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:1:5)


**כְּנִדְבוֹתָם נָדַר בְּנָזִיר וּבְקָרְבָּן.** אִם אָמַר כְּנִדְבוֹת כְּשֵׁרִים הֲרֵינִי נָזִיר וַהֲרֵי זֶה קָרְבָּן אִם אֹכַל כִּכָּר זוֹ, וַאֲכָלָהּ, חַיָּב בְּנָזִיר וּבְקָרְבָּן, שֶׁהַכְּשֵׁרִים פְּעָמִים נוֹדְרִים בְּנָזִיר לְאַפְרוֹשֵׁי מֵאִסּוּר. וּמִתְנַדְּבִים בְּקָרְבָּן שֶׁמְּבִיאִים קָרְבָּנָם לְפֶתַח הָעֲזָרָה וּמַקְדִּישִׁין אוֹתָהּ שָׁם כְּדֵי שֶׁלֹּא יָבֹאוּ בָהּ לִידֵי מִכְשׁוֹל. וּנְדָבָה הִיא כְּשֶׁיֹּאמַר הֲרֵי זוֹ. וְנֶדֶר, הֲרֵי עָלַי. לְפִיכָךְ הַכְּשֵׁרִים מִתְנַדְּבִים אֲבָל אֵינָם נוֹדְרִים, כִּי הֵיכִי דְלָא לֵיתוּ לִידֵי תַקָּלָה:


וכנדבותם נדר נזיר ובקרבן (as their free-will offerings – he has made a binding vow in the case of a Nazir or in the case of bringing an offering) – if he said, “like the free-will offerings of the suitable/appropriate [people], I will be a Nazir or this is an offering if I eat this loaf,” and he consumed it, he is liable regarding becoming a Nazir, or in bringing a sacrifice, for the suitable/appropriate people sometimes make vows of becoming a Nazir in order to separate from prohibition. But when they make a free-will donation of a sacrifice that they bring their offering to the entrance of the Temple courtyard and sanctify it there in order that they will now come through it as a hindrance/stumbling-block. And the free-will offering is when he says, “May this be,” or “A vow be upon me.” Therefore, the worthy people make a free-will offering but do not make a vow, in order that one may not be led to commit an offernse through it.



### Mishnah 2

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:2:1)


**קוֹנָם קוֹנָח קוֹנָס הֲרֵי אֵלּוּ כִנּוּיִין לְקָרְבָּן.** לְשׁוֹנוֹת שֶׁל גּוֹיִם הֵם, וְיֵשׁ מֵהֶם מִי שֶׁקּוֹרֵא לְקָרְבָּן כָּךְ וְיֵשׁ מִי שֶׁקּוֹרֵא כָּךְ, וּבְכָל לָשׁוֹן שֶׁיֹּאמַר מֵאֵלּוּ הוּא מַתְפִּיס בְּקָרְבָּן:


קונם קונח קונס הרי אלו כנויין לקרבן – these are the languages of non-Jews, and there are from them one who calls for a certain sacrifice, and there is one wo calls such, and in every language when he states from these [forms of language] he causes to take hold of a sacrifice.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:2:2)


**נָדַר בְּמוֹתָא.** כְּלוֹמַר נִשְׁבַּע בְּמוֹתָא, וְהוּא כִנּוּי שֶׁל מוֹמְתָא, שֶׁהִיא שְׁבוּעָה בִּלְשׁוֹן תַּרְגּוּם:


נדר במותא – meaning to say that he took an oath to curse, which is a substitute of an oath/imprecation, and it is a an oath in the language of an Aramaic translation



### Mishnah 3

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:1)


**לַחֻלִּין שֶׁאֹכַל לָךְ.** הַלָּמֶ"ד נְקֻדָּה פַתַּח, וּמַשְׁמַע לֹא חֻלִּין יִהְיֶה מַה שֶּׁאֹכַל לָךְ אֶלָּא קֹדֶשׁ:


לחולין שאוכל לך – The LAMED/ל has the vocalization of a PATACH, and it implies Something not unconsecrated will be what I will eat with you, but rather holy/sanctified.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:2)


**לֹא כָשֵׁר.** יִהְיֶה, אֶלָּא פָּסוּל. וְהַיְנוּ קָדָשִׁים דְּשַׁיָּךְ בְּהוּ כַּשְׁרוּת וּפַסְלוּת:


לא כשר – it will be, but rather invalid. And these are Holy Things that are connected with things that are fit/appropriate and invalid/inappropriate.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:3:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:3)


**וְלֹא דְכֵי.** לֹא מֻתָּר. כְּמוֹ אֵיל קַמְצָא דָכָן, בַּעֲבוֹדָה זָרָה [דַּף לז]. וְאַף עַל גַּב דִּלְשׁוֹן מֻתָּר וְאָסוּר שַׁיָּךְ נַמִּי גַבֵּי נְבֵלָה וּטְרֵפָה, וַאֲנַן קָיְמָא לָן שֶׁאֵין מַתְפִּיסִין אֶלָּא בְּדָבָר הַנִּדָּר וְהַנִּדָּב, הוֹאִיל וְיֵשׁ בְּמַשְׁמָעוּת מֻתָּר גַּם בְּקָדָשִׁים, הָא תְנַן סְתַם נְדָרִים לְהַחְמִיר, דְּכֵיוָן דְּדַעְתּוֹ לְהַתְפִּיסוֹ בְּנֶדֶר אָמְרִינַן עַל דָּבָר הַנִּדָּר נִתְכַּוֵּן:


לא דכי – not permitted, like a pure species of locust (see Tractate Eduyot, Chapter 8, Mishnah 4), in Tractate Avodah Zarah [37a – which is kosher, according to Rashi] and even though that the language of permitted and forbidden belongs also regarding a carrion that died of itself and an animal torn by a wild beast, but we hold that we don’t cause to take hold of it other than with something that is vowed or made as a free-will offering, for since there is an implication in “permitted” also in Holy Things, therefore it is taught in the Mishnah (Tractate Nedarim, Chapter 2, Mishnah 4): “Vows that are not spelled out are subject to a more stringent ruling,” for since it is his intention to cause to take hold of a vow, we state regarding a thing that is vowed he intended.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:3:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:4)


**טָהוֹר.** אִם אָמַר לֹא טָהוֹר מַה שֶּׁאֹכַל לָךְ:


טהור – if he stated: “it is not pure what I eat of yours.”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:3:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:5)


**טָמֵא נוֹתָר פִּגּוּל.** אִם אָמַר טָמֵא מַה שֶּׁאֹכַל לָךְ, וְכֵן כֻּלָּם: **אָסוּר.** שֶׁכָּל אֵלּוּ דְּבָרִים הַנּוֹהֲגִים בַּקָּדָשִׁים הֵם:


טמא נותר פיגול – if he said: “it is impure what I eat of yours,” and similarly for all of them. It is forbidden, that all of these things that are engaged with the Holy Things.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:3:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:6)


**כְּאִימְּרָא.** כַּשֶּׂה שֶׁל קָרְבָּן:


כאימרא – like the lamb/young sheep of a Sacrifice.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:3:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:7)


**כַּדִּירִים.** כְּלִשְׁכַּת הָעֵצִים אוֹ כְלִשְׁכַּת הַטְּלָאִים:


כדירים – like the chamber of wood or like the chamber of lambs.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:3:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:8)


**כָּעֵצִים.** כְּגִזְרֵי עֵצִים שֶׁל מַעֲרָכָה:


כעצים – like the pieces of wood of the pile of wood on he altar in the Temple.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:3:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:9)


**כָּאִשִּׁים.** כַּקָּרְבָּנוֹת שֶׁעַל הָאֵשׁ:


כאישים – like the sacrifices that are upon the fire.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:3:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:10)


**כַּמִּזְבֵּחַ.** כַּקָּרְבָּנוֹת שֶׁעַל גַּבֵּי הַמִּזְבֵּחַ:


כמזבח – like the sacrifices that are upon the altar.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:3:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:11)


**כַּהֵיכָל.** כַּקָּרְבָּנוֹת שֶׁבַּהֵיכָל:


כהיכל – like the sacrifices that are in the hall of the golden altar.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:3:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:12)


**כִּירוּשָׁלָיִם.** כַּקָּרְבָּנוֹת שֶׁבִּירוּשָׁלַיִם. פֵּרוּשׁ אַחֵר, כְּחוֹמוֹת יְרוּשָׁלַיִם, דְּקָסָבַר שֶׁחוֹמוֹת יְרוּשָׁלַיִם מִשְּׁיָרֵי הַלִּשְׁכָּה קָא אָתוּ:


כירושלים – like the sacrifices that are in Jerusalem. Another explanation: like the walls of Jeruslaem, for he holds that the walls of Jerusalem from the remnants of the chamber they have come.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:3:13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:13)


**בְּאֶחָד מִכָּל מְשַׁמְּשֵׁי הַמִּזְבֵּחַ.** כְּגוֹן מִזְלָגוֹת מִזְרָקוֹת וּמַחְתּוֹת. אִם אָמַר כַּמִּזְלָגוֹת שֶׁאֹכַל לָךְ, אוֹ כַמִּזְרָקוֹת שֶׁאֹכַל לָךְ, וְכֵן כֻּלָּם, אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁלֹּא הִזְכִּיר קָרְבָּן, הֲרֵי זֶה כְנוֹדֵר בְּקָרְבָּן:


באחד מכל משמשי המזבח (by one of the utensils used for the altar) – as, for example, the forks, bowls (out of which the sprinkling is done), coal-pans. If he said, “like the forks that I eat of yours,” or “like the bowls that I eat of yours,” and similarly for all of them, even though he didn’t mention [specifically], “sacrifice,” behold this is like making a vow for a sacrifice.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:3:14

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:14](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:3:14)


**רַבִּי יְהוּדָה אוֹמֵר הָאוֹמֵר יְרוּשָׁלַיִם.** בְּלֹא כָּ"ף, לֹא אָמַר כְּלוּם. וְתַנָּא קַמָּא פָּלֵיג עֲלֵיהּ. וְאֵין הֲלָכָה כְּרַבִּי יְהוּדָה:


רבי יהודה אומר האומר ירושלים – without the “KAF/כ ,” he did not say anything. But the first Tanna/teacher disputes him. And the Halakha is not according to Rabbi Yehuda.



### Mishnah 4

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:4:1)


**הָאוֹמֵר קָרְבָּן עוֹלָה מִנְחָה חַטָּאת תּוֹדָה וּשְׁלָמִים.** כָּל הָנֵי, קָרְבְּנוֹת חוֹבָה הֵם, וְתוֹדָה נַמִּי דָמְיָא לְחוֹבָה, דְּאַרְבָּעָה צְרִיכִין לְהוֹדוֹת. וְסָלְקָא דַעְתָּךְ אֲמִינָא דְּאֵין זֶה נוֹדֵר בְּדָבָר הַנָּדוּר:


האומר קרבן עולה מנחה חטאת תודה ושלמים – all of hese are obligatory sacrifices, and the thanksgiving-offering also is similar to an obligation, for four require givng thanks, but you might think I would say that this one takes a vow in something that is the legitimate subject of a vow.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:4:2)


**וְרַבִּי יְהוּדָה מַתִּיר.** מִשּׁוּם דַּאֲמָרָן בְּלֹא כָּ"ף דָּמֵי לְנִשְׁבָּע בְּחַיֵּי הַקָּרְבָּן וּבְחַיֵּי הָעוֹלָה וְאֵין כָּאן לֹא נֶדֶר וְלֹא שְׁבוּעָה. וְרֵישָׁא אַשְׁמוֹעִינַן דְּפָלֵיג תָּנָא קַמָּא עֲלֵיהּ דְּרַבִּי יְהוּדָה אֲפִלּוּ בִּירוּשָׁלַיִם אִם הִזְכִּירָהּ בְּלֹא כָּ"ף וְאָמַר דַּהֲוֵי נֶדֶר. וְסֵיפָא אַשְׁמוֹעִינַן דְּפָלֵיג רַבִּי יְהוּדָה עֲלֵיהּ דְּתַנָּא קַמָּא אֲפִלּוּ בְּקָרְבָּן עוֹלָה וּמִנְחָה וְכוּ'‏ כְּשֶׁהִזְכִּירָן בְּלֹא כָּ"ף דְּלֹא הָוֵי נֶדֶר:


ורבי יהודה מתיר – because they were said without the "כ"/KAF they are compared to someone who took an oath regarding the life of the burnt offering, and thee isn’t here either a vow nor an oath. But the first clause [of the Mishnah] teaches us that the first Tanna/teacher disputes that of Rabbi Yehuda, even regarding Jerusalem if he mentioned it without the "כ"/KAF and stated that it is a vow. But the concluding clause [of the Mishnah] comes to inform us that Rabbi Yehuda disputes that [opinion] of the first Tanna/Teacher, even with a sacrifice of a burnt-offering, etc., when he mentioned them without a "כ" /KAF – for it is not a vow.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:4:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:4:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:4:3)


**קָרְבָּן הַקָּרְבָּן כְּקָרְבָּן שֶׁאֹכַל לָךְ אָסוּר.** אַף עַל גַּב דְּכָל הָנֵי שָׁמְעִינַן לְהוּ כְבָר, הַקָּרְבָּן אִצְטְרִיכָא לֵיהּ, דְּסָלְקָא דַעְתָּךְ אֲמִינָא בְּחַיֵּי הַקָּרְבָּן קָאָמַר. וְהָא דִּתְנַן לְקַמָּן בְּפֶרֶק ב'‏ הַקָּרְבָּן שֶׁאֹכַל לָךְ מֻתָּר, הָתָם הָא קָרְבָּן קָאָמַר, דְּמַשְׁמַע חַיֵּי קָרְבָּן:


קרבן הקרבו כקרבן שאוכל לך אסור (May what I eat of yours be the Korban” “like the Korban,” [By] a Korban [do I vow] be what I eat with you, he is bound) – even though we we heard all of them already, “the KORBAN” is required for it, for you might I would say that he says, “By the life of the KORBAN.” But surely it is taught in the Mishnah further on in Chapter 2 [Mishnah 2], “Korban” be what I eat with you,” he is not bound, there it is speaking of a KORBAN/sacrifice, which implies the life of the Korban.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:4:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:4:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:4:4)


**לְקָרְבָּן לֹא אֹכַל לָךְ רַבִּי מֵאִיר אוֹסֵר.** דְּנַעֲשָׂה כְאוֹמֵר לְקָרְבָּן יְהֵא לְפִיכָךְ לֹא אֹכַל לָךְ. וְאֵין הֲלָכָה כְּרַבִּי מֵאִיר:


לקרבן אל אוכל לך רבי מאיר אוסר (for a KORBAN shall be what I eat with you) – that it is made like saying, “it shall be like a sacrifice,” therefore, I will not eat with you. But the Halakha is not according to Rabbi Meir.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 1:4:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:4:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/1:4:5)


**קוֹנָם פִּי מְדַבֵּר עִמָּךְ.** וְאַף עַל גַּב דְּאֵין נְדָרִים חָלִים עַל דָּבָר שֶׁאֵין בּוֹ מַמָּשׁ, וְדִבּוּר אֵין בּוֹ מַמָּשׁ, מִכָּל מָקוֹם כְּשֶׁאָמַר קוֹנָם פִּי מְדַבֵּר עִמָּךְ אָסַר הַפֶּה מִלְּדַבֵּר, וְהַפֶּה דָּבָר שֶׁיֵּשׁ בּוֹ מַמָּשׁ הוּא. וְכֵן יֵאָסְרוּ יָדַי לְמַעֲשֵׂיהֶם, וְרַגְלַי לְהִלּוּכָן, וְכָל כַּיּוֹצֵא בָּזֶה:


קונם פי מדבר עמך – but even though that the vows do not take effect on a matter lacking substance, and speech has no substance in it, nevertheless, when he says, “KONAM be my mouth with speaks to you,” he prohibits his mouth from speaking, and his mouth spoke something that has substance. And similarly, “my hands be forbidden from their actions,” and/or “my feet from their walking,” and all similar things to this.



## Chapter 2


### Mishnah 1

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:1:1)


**וְאֵלּוּ מֻתָּרִין.** חֻלִּין שֶׁאֹכַל לָךְ. לְסִימָן בְּעָלְמָא נַקְטֵיהּ, כְּשֵׁם שֶׁחֻלִּין שֶׁאֹכַל לָךְ אֵין צָרִיךְ שְׁאֵלָה לֶחָכָם, כָּךְ כָּל הָנֵי דְּאָמְרִינַן בְּמַתְנִיתִין בְּרֵישָׁא אֵין צְרִיכִין שְׁאֵלַת חָכָם:


ואלו מותרין. חולין שאוכל לך (and these [vows] are not binding: may what I eat of yours be unconsecrated food) – he (i.e., the teacher of the Mishnah) took these merely as a sign, ust as unconsecrated food that I may eat of yours – there is no need for a request made to a Sage (i.e., if a person makes a vow or takes an oath, or consecrates property, and afterwards regrets having done so, he may go to a Sage and request that he release him from his vow). So also, all of these that we mention in our Mishnah in the first clause there is no need for a request made to a Sage.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:1:2)


**כְּעוֹרוֹת לְבוּבִין.** הָיוּ נוֹקְבִים הַבְּהֵמָה מֵחַיִּים כְּנֶגֶד הַלֵּב וּמוֹצִיאִין הַלֵּב וּמַקְרִיבִין אוֹתוֹ לַעֲבוֹדָה זָרָה, וְתִקְרֹבֶת עֲבוֹדָה זָרָה אָסוּר בַּהֲנָאָה:


כעורות לבובין (like hides pierced at the heart) – they wud make incisions in the animal while it is living opposite the heart and remove the heart and sacrifice it to idolatry, and this sacrifice to idolatry is prohibited to derive any benefit.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:1:3)


**כְּחַלַּת אַהֲרֹן.** שֶׁהָיָה רִאשׁוֹן לַכֹּהֲנִים. וְלֹא הָוֵי דָּבָר הַנָּדוּר, שֶׁאֵין חַלָּה וּתְרוּמָה בָּאִין בְּנֶדֶר וּנְדָבָה:


כחלת אהרן – who was first for the Kohanim (see Numbers 15:20 – “You shall make a gift to the LORD from the first yield of your baking, throughout the ages). And it cannot be something that is vowed, for Hallah and heave-offering/Terumah come as a vow or a free-will offering.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:1:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:1:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:1:4)


**הֲרֵי זֶה מֻתָּר.** דְּאָמַר קְרָא (במדבר ל) אִישׁ כִּי יִדֹּר נֶדֶר, עַד שֶׁיִּדֹּר בְּדָבָר הַנָּדוּר. וְחַטָּאת שֶׁאֵינִי אוֹכֵל לָךְ דְּאָמְרִינַן בְּפֶרֶק קַמָּא דְּאָסוּר וְאַף עַל פִּי שֶׁאֵינוֹ דָּבָר הַנָּדוּר, הָוֵי טַעֲמָא, דְּאֶפְשָׁר שֶׁיָּבִיא חַטָּאת עַל יְדֵי נֶדֶר, כְּגוֹן שֶׁנָּדַר בְּנָזִיר וּמִתְחַיֵּב לְהָבִיא חַטָּאת:


הרי זה מותר – as it states in Scripture (Numbers 30:3): “If a man makes a vow [to the LORD],” until he should make a vow with a thing that is vowed. But a sin-offering that I won’t eat with you , as we stated in the first chapter [of Tractate Nedarim, Mishnah 4], even though it is not something that is vowed, there is reason, for it is possible that he will bring a sin-offering through a vow, as for example, that he took a vow to be a Nazir, and is liable to bring a sin-offering [upon the conclusion of the thirty-day length of serving as a Nazir].


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:1:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:1:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:1:5)


**הֲרֵי אַתְּ עָלַי כְּאִמָּא.** אַף עַל גַּב דְּלָאו דָּבָר הַנָּדוּר הוּא, חָמוּר מִכָּל הָנֵי דִּלְעֵיל, דְּצָרִיךְ הֶתֵּרָא מִדְּרַבָּנָן אִם הוּא עַם הָאָרֶץ:


הרי את עלי כאמא (Lo, you are like mother to me) – even though this this is not a matter that is vowed, it is more severe from all of the others that are above, he requires a release from the Rabbis if he is an illiterate individual (i.e., “Am HaAretz)


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:1:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:1:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:1:6)


**פּוֹתְחִין לוֹ פֶתַח מִמָּקוֹם אַחֵר.** כְּלוֹמַר מְבַקְּשִׁים לוֹ פֶּתַח וְטַעַם לַחֲרָטָתוֹ. וְלֹא סַגִּי בִכְדּוֹ תָּהִית אוֹ לִבָּךְ עֲלָךְ. וְכָל זֶה שֶׁלֹּא יָקֵל רֹאשׁוֹ וְלֹא יַרְגִּיל לֶאֱסֹר אִשְׁתּוֹ עָלָיו:


and we make an opening for retracting a vow from another place (i.e., reason) meaning to say, we request for him and opening and reason for his remorse. But it is not sufficient [to ask him]: Do you now regret [that you had vowed]? Or that your heart is upon you – that you bear a grudge (see Tractate Nedarim 21b), and all of this in order that he may not behave lightly in such a matter and that he should not become accustomed to forbid his wife upon him.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:1:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:1:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:1:7)


**קוֹנָם שֶׁאֵינִי יָשֵׁן כוּ'.** הֲרֵי זֶה בְּבַל יַחֵל מִדְּרַבָּנָן. אֲבָל מִן הַתּוֹרָה אֵין הַנֶּדֶר חָל, דִּנְדָרִים אֵין חָלִים אֶלָּא עַל דָּבָר שֶׁיֵּשׁ בּוֹ מַמָּשׁ:


קונם שאיני ישן כו' – Lo, this is (Numbers 30:3): “he shall not break his pledge” according to the Rabbis, but from the Torah, there is no vow taking effect, for vows do not take effect other than on something that has substance (see Talmud Nedarim 15a).


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:1:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:1:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:1:8)


**קוֹנָם שֶׁאֵינִי מְשַׁמְּשֵׁךְ.** בַּגְּמָרָא מַקְשֶׁה, וְהָא מְשֻׁעְבָּד לָהּ וְהֵיאַךְ יָכוֹל לְהַפְקִיעַ שִׁעְבּוּדָהּ בְּנִדְרוֹ, וְהָא הָוֵי כְאוֹסֵר פֵּרוֹת חֲבֵרוֹ עַל חֲבֵרוֹ. וּמְשָׁנֵי, כְּגוֹן דְּאָמַר הֲנָאַת תַּשְׁמִישֵׁךְ עָלַי קוֹנָם, שֶׁאָסַר הַהֲנָאָה עָלָיו, וְאֵין מַאֲכִילִין לוֹ לָאָדָם דָּבָר הָאָסוּר לוֹ:


קונם שאיני משמשך (that I will not have sexual relations with you) – In the Gemara (Tractate Nedarim 15b) a difficulty is raised if he is subservient to her, how is he able to release her subservience through his vow, for it is like prohibiting the produce of his fellow on his fellow? But it [i.e., the Gemara] responds, as, for example, when he stated that he takes a vow against enjoying the benefit of her sexual relations with me,” that he forbade this benefit upon himself, and we don’t feed a person something that is forbidden to him.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:1:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:1:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:1:9)


**שְׁבוּעָה שֶׁאֵינִי יָשֵׁן וְכוּ'.** אָסוּר מִן הַתּוֹרָה, דִּשְׁבוּעוֹת חָלִין בֵּין עַל דָּבָר שֶׁיֵּשׁ בּוֹ מַמָּשׁ בֵּין עַל דָּבָר שֶׁאֵין בּוֹ מַמָּשׁ. וְאִם נִשְׁבַּע שֶׁלֹּא יִישַׁן שְׁלֹשָׁה יָמִים רְצוּפִין לַיְלָה וָיוֹם, לוֹקֶה וְיִישַׁן מִיָּד, מִפְּנֵי שֶׁנִּשְׁבַּע עַל דָּבָר שֶׁאִי אֶפְשָׁר לְקַיְּמוֹ:


שבועה שאיני ישן וכו' – this is forbidden form the Torah, for oaths take effect whether there is something in it of substance, or whether it is on something that has no substance. But if he took an oath to not sleep for three consecutive days, night and day, he is flogged/whipped and he sleeps immediately, because he took an oath on something that is impossible to fulfill.



### Mishnah 2

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:2:1)


**קָרְבָּן לֹא אֹכַל לָךְ כוּ'.** מֻתָּר, דַּהֲוֵי כְנִשְׁבָּע בַּקָּרְבָּן וְנַעֲשָׂה כְאוֹמֵר בְּחַיֵּי הַקָּרְבָּן אִם אֹכַל לָךְ שׁוּם דָּבָר:


קרבן לא אכל לך כו' (KORBAN,I shall not eat with you) – he is permitted (i.e., he is not bound), for this would be like he took an oath with a KORBAN that he would become like he says: “by my Life, a KORBAN if I eat with anything with you.”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:2:2)


**שְׁבוּעָה שֶׁלֹּא אֹכַל לָךְ.** וְלֹא אָמְרִינַן בְּחַיֵּי שְׁבוּעָה קָאָמַר כִּדְאָמְרִינַן בְּקָרְבָּן, דִּשְׁבוּעָה לֵית בָּהּ מְשָׁשָׁא וְלֹא שַׁיָּךְ לוֹמַר בָּהּ בְּחַיֵּי שְׁבוּעָה:


שבועה שלא אוכל לך – and we don’t say, “by my life with an oath it is said,” as we say with a KORBAN, for an oath has no substance and it doesn’t belong to say with it, “by my life with an oath.”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:2:3)


**שְׁבוּעָה שֶׁאֹכַל לָךְ.** זִמְנִין דִּשְׁבוּעָה שֶׁאֹכַל לָךְ דְּלֹא אָכִילְנָא מַשְׁמַע, כְּגוֹן שֶׁהָיָה חֲבֵרוֹ מְסָרֵב בּוֹ לֶאֱכֹל וְאָמַר לֹא אָכִילְנָא לֹא אָכִילְנָא, וְשׁוּב אָמַר שְׁבוּעָה שֶׁאֹכַל לָךְ, דְּלֹא אָכִילְנָא מַשְׁמַע, וְהָכִי קָאָמַר, בִּשְׁבוּעָה יְהֵא עָלַי אִי אָכִילְנָא לָךְ:


שבועה שאוכל לך – sometimes, “the taking of an oath that I shall eat with you” is not that I will actually eat, as, for example, that his fellow would refuse him to him and he says, “I will not eat, I will not eat,” and thereafter he says, “by an oath I will eat with y,” that he will not actually eat,” and this is what he said, “with an oath that will be upon me, if I eat with you.”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:2:4)


**זֶה חֹמֶר בַּשְּׁבוּעוֹת.** לֹא מָצִינַן לְאוֹקְמַהּ אַשְּׁבוּעָה שֶׁלֹּא אֹכַל לָךְ וְכוּ', דְּמִדְּקָתָנֵי זֶה, חֹמֶר מַשְׁמַע, דְּנֶדֶר הָוֵי אֶלָּא דְּאֵינוֹ חָמוּר כִּשְׁבוּעָה, וְגַבֵּי קָרְבָּן לֹא אֹכַל לָךְ תְּנַן מֻתָּר, דְּלֹא הָוֵי נֶדֶר כְּלָל. מִשּׁוּם הָכִי צָרִיךְ לְאוֹקְמַהּ אַדִּלְעֵיל דִּתְנִינָא קוֹנָם שֶׁאֵינִי יָשֵׁן שֶׁאֵינִי מְדַבֵּר הֲרֵי זֶה בְּבַל יַחֵל, וְאוֹקִימְנָא מִדְּרַבָּנָן, דְּמִדְּאוֹרַיְתָא אֵין הַנֶּדֶר חָל אֶלָּא עַל דָּבָר שֶׁיֵּשׁ בּוֹ מַמָּשׁ, וְזֶה חֹמֶר בַּשְּׁבוּעוֹת מִבַּנְּדָרִים, שֶׁשְּׁבוּעָה חָלָה אֲפִלּוּ עַל דָּבָר שֶׁאֵין בּוֹ מַמָּשׁ:


זה חומר בשבועות – we are not able to maintain an oath that I shall not eat with you, for since it teaches that this is more stringent, it implies that it is a vow, but it is not more stringent like an an oath, and regarding “Korban, I will not eat with you,” it is taught in the Mishnah that he is not bound, and that it is not a vow at all. Because of this, one needs to establish it on what is taught above (Chapter 2, Mishnah 1): “KONAM that I shall not sleep,” “that I won’t speak,” for behold, this [applies] to (Numbers 30:3): “he shall not break his pledge.” But we establish from the Rabbis that according to the Torah, the vow has not effect other than on something which has substance, and this is the stringency regarding oaths from that of vows, that an oath takes effect even on something that has no substance.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:2:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:2:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:2:5)


**תְּפִלִּין שֶׁאֲנִי מֵנִיחַ.** מִשּׁוּם דְּאָסַר חֶפְצָא עֲלֵיהּ, וְאֵינוֹ נִרְאֶה כְּנוֹדֵר לְבַטֵּל אֶת הַמִּצְוָה, שֶׁהֲרֵי לֹא קִבֵּל עַל עַצְמוֹ אֶלָּא אָסַר הַחֵפֶץ עָלָיו, וְאִם יְקַיֵּם הַמִּצְוָה הַוְיָא מִצְוָה הַבָּאָה בַּעֲבֵרָה, וְדָמֵי לְמִי שֶׁחַיָּב לֶאֱכֹל מַצָּה בְּלֵילֵי פֶסַח וְלֹא מָצָא אֶלָּא מַצָּה שֶׁל טֶבֶל אוֹ שֶׁל הֶקְדֵּשׁ, שֶׁאָסוּר לְאָכְלָהּ. אֲבָל כָּל לְשׁוֹן שְׁבוּעָה הוּא אוֹסֵר אֶת עַצְמוֹ מִלַּעֲשׂוֹת הַדָּבָר, וְכֵיוָן שֶׁהוּא מְחֻיָּב לַעֲשׂוֹת הַמִּצְוָה לָאו כֹּל כְּמִינֵיהּ לְהַפְקִיעַ עַצְמוֹ מֵחִיּוּב הַמִּצְוָה. וְאִם אָמַר הֲרֵי עָלַי קָרְבָּן אִם אָנִיחַ תְּפִלִּין, חָל הַנֶּדֶר וְחַיָּב לְהָבִיא קָרְבָּן אִם הֵנִיחַ תְּפִלִּין:


תפילין שאני מניח – because he forbid a sacred object held in hand at the delivery of an oath that is upon him, and it doesn’t appear like taking a vow to void the Mitzvah, for behold, he didn’t accepted it upon himself, but rather, prohibited the sacred object upon him, but he would fulfill the Mitzvah it would a Mitzvah that comes to be fulfilled by means of a transgression and it would be similar to someone who is required to eat Mitzvah on the nights of Passover but only found Matzah of eatables forbidden pending the separation of sacred gifts or something dedicated to the Temple, which is forbidden to consumel. But all languages of oath that he forbids upon himself from doing something, for since he is liable to perform the Mitzvah, it is not within his powers to release himself from the obligation of the commandment. But if he said, “a KORBAN is upon me if I put on Tefillin, the vow takes affect and he is liable to bring a sacrifice if he put on Tefillin.



### Mishnah 3

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:3:1)


**חַיָּב עַל כָּל אַחַת וְאֶחָת.** וְיִהְיֶה נָזִיר שְׁלֹשִׁים יוֹם אִם אָמַר הֲרֵינִי נָזִיר סְתָם, וְיָבִיא קָרְבַּן נָזִיר, וְיַחְזֹר לִהְיוֹת נָזִיר כְּמִסְפַּר הַפְּעָמִים שֶׁאָמַר שֶׁיִּהְיֶה נָזִיר. וּבִשְׁבוּעָה אֵינוֹ חַיָּב אֶלָּא אַחַת, שֶׁאֵינוֹ לוֹקֶה אֶלָּא מַלְקוּת אַחַת. אֲבָל אִם נִשְׁאַל עַל הַשְּׁבוּעָה הָרִאשׁוֹנָה, חָלָה הַשְּׁבוּעָה הַשְּׁנִיָּה, וְכֵן אִם נִשְׁאַל עַל הַשְּׁנִיָּה, חָלָה הַשְּׁלִישִׁית, וְאָסוּר לֶאֱכֹל עַד שֶׁיֻּתְּרוּ כֻּלָּם, מִדְּלֹא תְּנַן הֲרֵי זוֹ שְׁבוּעָה אַחַת, אֶלָּא אֵינוֹ חַיָּב אֶלָּא אַחַת:


חייב על כל אחת ואחת – and he will a Nazir for thirty days if he said, I am an undefined Nazir, nad he will bring the Nazirite sacrifice and return to be a Nazir according to the number of times that he said, “I will be a Nazir.” But regarding an oath, he is not liable other than for one [sacrifice,” for he is not flogged/whipped other than one set of stripes. But if he made a request to be released on the first oath, the second oath takes effect. And similarly, if he made a request to be released on the second [oath], the third takes effect. But he is is forbidden to eat until he all of them are not binding, because it does not teach in the Mishnah, “behold this is one oath,” but rather, “he is only liable for one count only.”



### Mishnah 4

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:4:1)


**וּפֵרוּשָׁם לְהָקֵל.** אַף עַל גַּב דִּכְשֶׁפֵּרֵשׁ דְּבָרָיו אָזְלִינַן בָּתַר פֵּרוּשׁוֹ, כִּי סָתַם וְלֹא פֵּרֵשׁ אָזְלִינַן לְהַחְמִיר, דִּסְתָם נוֹדֵר דַּעְתּוֹ לְאִסּוּר:


ופירושם להקל – even though that when he explained his words, we follow after his explanation, for when it is undefined and he didn’t explain, we go to the most stringent [opinion], for a person who makes an undefined vow, his intention is to forbid.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:4:2)


**כְּבָשָׂר מָלִיחַ.** הַיְנוּ קָרְבָּן, דִּכְתִיב (ויקרא ב) עַל כָּל קָרְבָּנְךָ תַּקְרִיב מֶלַח:


כבשר מליח – which is a sacrifice, as it is written (Leviticus 2:13): “[You shall season your every offering of grain with salt; you shall not omit from your grain offering the salt of your covenant with God;] with all your offerings you must offer salt.”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:4:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:4:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:4:3)


**כְּחֵרֶם שֶׁל כֹּהֲנִים מֻתָּר.** אַף עַל גַּב דְּחֶרְמֵי כֹהֲנִים מוֹעֲלִים בָּהֶם עַד שֶׁיָּבֹאוּ לִידֵי כֹהֲנִים וְהָוֵי דָבָר הַנָּדוּר, מִכָּל מָקוֹם סְתַם חֶרְמֵי כֹהֲנִים מַשְׁמַע שֶׁכְּבָר בָּאוּ לְיַד כֹּהֵן:


כחרם של כהנים מותר (assigned to the private use of priests) – even though that which belongs to the priests, people commit religious sacrilege with them until they come into the hands of the Kohanim, and they are a thing that is vowed, nevertheless, undefined property of the priests implies that they have already come into the hand of a Kohen (see also Tractate Arakhin, Chapter 8, Mishnah 6)..


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:4:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:4:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:4:4)


**אִם כְּמַעֲשַׂר בְּהֵמָה אָסוּר.** דַּהֲוֵי דָּבָר הַנָּדוּר, שֶׁצָּרִיךְ לְהַקְדִּישׁוֹ, וְאֵין מַעֲשַׂר בְּהֵמָה אוֹסֵר הַדִּיר כְּמוֹ שֶׁמַּעֲשַׂר דָּגָן אוֹסֵר הַגֹּרֶן:


אם כמעשר בהמה אסור – for this is something that was vowed, that one must sanctify it, but the tithe of cattle does not prohibit the stable/shed like the tithing of grain prohibits the granary/threshing floor.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:4:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:4:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:4:5)


**שֶׁאֵין אַנְשֵׁי גָלִיל מַכִּירִין תְּרוּמַת הַלִּשְׁכָּה.** לְפִי שֶׁהָיוּ רְחוֹקִים מִירוּשָׁלַיִם:


שאין אנשי גליל מכירין תרומת הלשכה – becase they were far from Jerusalem.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:4:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:4:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:4:6)


**שֶׁאֵין אַנְשֵׁי גָלִיל מַכִּירִין חֶרְמֵי כֹהֲנִים.** וְכָל מַה שֶּׁהָיוּ מַחְרִימִין הָיוּ מַפְרִישִׁין לְבֶדֶק הַבַּיִת. וַהֲלָכָה כְּרַבִּי יְהוּדָה:


שין אנשי גליל מכירין חרמי כהנים – for everything that they would dedicate for priestly use, they would set aside for the repair of the Temple. And the Halakha is according to Rabbi Yehuda.



### Mishnah 5

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:5:1)


**בְּחֶרְמוֹ שֶׁל יָם.** לְשׁוֹן מְצוּדָה, כְּמוֹ אֲשֶׁר הִיא מְצוֹדִים וַחֲרָמִים (קהלת ז):


בחרמו של ים – the language of a net, as in (Ecclesiastes 7:26): “[Now, I find women more bitter than death,] she is all traps , her hands are fetters, [and her heart is snares].”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:5:2)


**הֲרֵי עַצְמִי קָרְבָּן.** אָסַר עַצְמוֹ כְּקָרְבָּן עַל חֲבֵרוֹ:


הרי עצמי קרבן – he forbade himself like a KORBAN/sacrifice upon his fellow.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:5:3)


**אֵין נִשְׁאָלִים עֲלֵיהֶם.** אֵין צְרִיכִין שְׁאֵלַת חָכָם, שֶׁאֵינָן חָלִים:


אין נשאלים עליהם – it is not necessary for a request to a Sage [to release him from a vow] for they don’t take effect.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:5:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:5:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:5:4)


**וְאִם נִשְׁאֲלוּ.** וְאִם עַם הָאָרֶץ הוּא זֶה שֶׁנָּדַר כְּהַאי גַּוְנָא וּבָא לִשָּׁאֵל עַל נִדְרוֹ, אֵין פּוֹתְחִין לוֹ פֶּתַח לַחֲרָטָה וְאֵין מַתִּירִין לוֹ. וְאִם עָבַר עַל נִדְרוֹ זֶה מְנַדִּין אוֹתוֹ:


ואם נשאלין – but if this person is an illiterate person/Am HaAretz who made a vow in such a manner and he came to request from a Sage [to release him] from his vow, we don’t make an opening for him for regret and we don’t release him. But if he transgressed on this vow, we excommunicate him.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 2:5:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:5:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/2:5:5)


**פּוֹתְחִין לוֹ פֶתַח מִמָּקוֹם אַחֵר.** מַרְאִים לוֹ שֶׁהַנֶּדֶר קַיָּם וּפוֹתְחִין לוֹ פֶּתַח מִטַּעַם אַחֵר וּמַתִּירִין לוֹ נִדְרוֹ. אֲבָל לֹא עוֹנְשִׁין וְלֹא מַחְמִירִין. וְכֵן הֲלָכָה:


פוחתין לו פתח ממקום אחר – we show him that the matter exists and we make an opening for him for another reason and release him from his vow. But we don’t punish him and we are not stringent with him. And such is the Halakha.



## Chapter 3


### Mishnah 1

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:1:1)


**אַרְבָּעָה נְדָרִים.** כֻּלְּהוּ מְפָרֵשׁ לְהוּ וְאָזֵיל:


ארבעה נדרים (vows intended for urging to buy/sell or while bargaining; vows made dependent upon an impossibility/an exaggeration; vows made in error; vows made on conditions unavoidably fulfilled/under compulsion) – all of them will be explained further on.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:1:2)


**קוֹנָם שֶׁאֵינִי פוֹחֵת לְךָ מִן הַסֶּלַע.** קוֹנָם כִּכָּר זֶה עָלַי אִם אֲנִי פּוֹחֵת לְךָ מִן הַסֶּלַע. וְהַסֶּלַע הוּא אַרְבָּעָה דִּינָרִים:


קונם שאני פוחת לך מן מסלע – KONAM this loaf of bread if I [pay] less to you than a Sela. And a SELA is four Denarim.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:1:3)


**עַל הַשֶּׁקֶל.** הוּא חֲצִי סֶלַע:


על השקל – it is one-half of a SELA.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:1:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:1:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:1:4)


**שְׁנֵיהֶם רוֹצִים בִּשְׁלֹשָׁה דִינָרִים.** וְלֹא הָיָה בְּלִבָּם לְשֵׁם נֶדֶר, אֶלָּא הַמּוֹכֵר נָדַר לְזָרֵז הַלּוֹקֵחַ שֶׁיּוֹסִיף דָּמִים, וְכֵן הַלּוֹקֵחַ כְּדֵי שֶׁיִּפְחֹת הַמּוֹכֵר בִּדְמֵי הַמִּקָּח, הִלְכָּךְ לֹא הָוֵי נֶדֶר. וְאַף עַל גַּב דִּדְבָרִים שֶׁבַּלֵּב אֵינָם דְּבָרִים, הֵיכָא דְמוּכְחָא מִלְּתָא כִּי הָכָא, שֶׁכֵּן דֶּרֶךְ כָּל מוֹכְרִין וְלוֹקְחִין לַעֲשׂוֹת כֵּן, אָזְלִינַן בָּתַר דְּבָרִים שֶׁבַּלֵּב:


שניהם רוצים בשלשה דינרים – but it was not in their hearts for the sake of a vow, but rather, the seller vowed to urge/encourage the purchaser that he should increase they money, and similarly, the purchaser, in order that the seller should lessen the cost of the purchase, therefore, it was not a vow. But even though that matters that are in the heart are not matters, where a matter can be proven like here, for such is the manner of all sellers and purchasers to do this, we follow after the things that are in the heart.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:1:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:1:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:1:5)


**רַבִּי אֱלִיעֶזֶר בֶּן יַעֲקֹב אוֹמֵר אַף הָרוֹצֶה לְהַדִּיר אֶת חֲבֵרוֹ כוּ'.** מְפָרֵשׁ בַּגְּמָרָא דְּחַסּוֹרֵי מִחַסְּרָא וְהָכִי קָתָנֵי, הָרוֹצֶה שֶׁיֹּאכַל חֲבֵרוֹ אֶצְלוֹ וּמְסָרֵב בּוֹ וּמַדִּירוֹ, נִדְרֵי זֵרוּזִין הוּא. וְהָרוֹצֶה שֶׁלֹּא יִתְקַיְּמוּ נְדָרָיו כָּל הַשָּׁנָה יַעֲמֹד בְּרֹאשׁ הַשָּׁנָה, וְיֹאמַר, כָּל נֶדֶר שֶׁאֲנִי עָתִיד לִדֹּר יְהֵא בָטֵל. וְיַעֲמֹד בְּרֹאשׁ הַשָּׁנָה לָאו דַּוְקָא, אֶלָּא הוּא הַדִּין בְּכָל עֵת שֶׁיִּרְצֶה וּלְכָל זְמַן שֶׁיִּקְבַּע:


ר"א בן יעקב אומר אף כל הרוצה להדיר את חבירו כו' – The Gemara (Tractate Nedarim 23b) that our Mishnah is deficient and should be read as follows: A person who wants that his fellow should eat with him but refuses him and forces him to take a vow, it is the vow intended for urging to buy/sell. And the person that wants that he will not vows will not be fulfilled all year will stand up on Rosh Hashanah and state, “every vow that I will someday make is nullified.” And that he will stand up on Rosh Hashanah is not exact, but rather, the same law applies at every time that he wishes and for every time that he establishes.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:1:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:1:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:1:6)


**וּבִלְבַד שֶׁיִּהְיֶה זָכוּר.** מִן הַתְּנַאי בִּשְׁעַת הַנֶּדֶר וְדַעְתּוֹ עַל הַתְּנַאי שֶׁיִּהְיֶה קַיָּם, אָז הַנֶּדֶר בָּטֵל. אֲבָל אִם לֹא נִזְכַּר מִן הַתְּנַאי בִּשְׁעַת הַנֶּדֶר וְלֹא בְּתוֹךְ כְּדֵי דִּבּוּר מִשָּׁעָה שֶׁנָּדַר, הַנֶּדֶר קַיָּם. וְאֵין צָרִיךְ לוֹמַר אִם נִזְכַּר מִן הַתְּנַאי בִּשְׁעַת הַנֶּדֶר וְדַעְתּוֹ שֶׁיִּהְיֶה הַתְּנַאי בָּטֵל וְהַנֶּדֶר קַיָּם, דִּפְשִׁיטָא שֶׁהַנֶּדֶר קַיָּם. וּמִשְׁפְּטֵי הַשְּׁבוּעוֹת וְהַנְּדָרִים שָׁוִין לְדִין זֶה. וַהֲלָכָה כְּרַבִּי אֱלִיעֶזֶר בֶּן יַעֲקֹב:


ובלבד שיהיה זכור – from the condition at the time of the vow, and his mind/intention is upon the condition that it will be fulfilled, then the vow is nullified. But if he is not reminded of the condition at the time of the vow and not at an interval equivalent to the time of speaking (i.e., the amount of time it takes to say in Hebrew, “Greetings to you, my teacher and rabbi”), from the time that he made the vow, the vow exists/endures. But it is not necessary to state that if he is reminded from the condition at the time of the vow and it is his intention that the condition will be nullified and vow existent, for it is clear that the vow exists. But the laws of oaths and vows are equivalent in this law. And the Halakha is according to Rabbi Eliezer ben Yaakov.



### Mishnah 2

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:2:1)


**נִדְרֵי הֲבַאי.** גֻּזְמָא וּשְׂפַת יֶתֶר, וְהוּא בְּעַצְמוֹ יוֹדֵעַ שֶׁלֹּא הָיָה כָּךְ:


נדרי הבאי – exaggeration and additional speech, but he himself knows that it was not the case.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:2:2)


**קוֹנָם אִם לֹא רָאִיתִי.** קוֹנָם עָלַי כִּכָּר זֶה אִם לֹא רָאִיתִי וְכוּ':


קונם אם לא ראיתי – KONAM upon me this loaf of bread if I did no see, etc. (i.e., a snake as s big as the beam of an olive press).


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:2:3)


**נִדְרֵי שְׁגָגוֹת.** אָמַר קוֹנָם עָלַי כִּכָּר זֶה:


נדרי שגגות – he said, KONAM upon me this loaf of bread.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:2:4)


**אִם אָכַלְתִּי אִם שָׁתִיתִי וְנִזְכַּר שֶׁאָכַל וְשָׁתָה.** וּבִשְׁעַת הַנֶּדֶר הָיָה סָבוּר שֶׁלֹּא אָכַל וְלֹא שָׁתָה, לֹא הָוֵי נֶדֶר:


אם אכלתי אם שתיתי ונזכר שאכל ושתה – and at the time of the vow he thought that he did not eat and did not drink, it would not be a vow.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:2:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:2:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:2:5)


**קוֹנָם שֶׁאֵינִי אוֹכֵל לָךְ וְאֵינִי שׁוֹתֶה, וְשָׁכַח וְאָכַל וְשָׁתָה.** דִּבְשָׁעָה שֶׁהַנֶּדֶר חָל שֶׁהוּא בִשְׁעַת הָאֲכִילָה וְהַשְּׁתִיָּה שָׁכַח אֶת הַנֶּדֶר, מֻתָּר, דְּיָלְפִינַן מִשְּׁבוּעָה דִּכְתִיב (ויקרא ה) הָאָדָם בִּשְׁבוּעָה, דְּבָעִינַן שֶׁיִּהְיֶה אָדָם בְּשָׁעָה שֶׁהַשְּׁבוּעָה חָלָה עָלָיו, כְּלוֹמַר שֶׁיִּהְיֶה זָכוּר מִן הַשְּׁבוּעָה, וְהוּא הַדִּין בְּנֶדֶר:


קונם שאיני אוכלך לך ואיני שותה, ושכח ואכל ושתה – that at the time that the vow takes place which is at the time of eating and/or drinking, he forgot the vow, it is permitted/annulled, as we derive it from an oath as it is written (Leviticus 5:4): “[Or when a person uttes an oath to bad or good purpose –] whatever a man may utter in an oath – [and though he has known it, the fact has escaped him, but later he realizes his guilt in any of these matters -]” that we require that he will be a man at the time when the oath takes place upon him, meaning to say, that he will be mindful from the oath, and the same law applies with regard to a vow.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:2:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:2:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:2:6)


**קוֹנָם אִשְׁתִּי נֶהֱנֵית לִי שֶׁגָּנְבָה אֶת כִּיסִי.** הַיְנוּ נִדְרֵי שְׁגָגוֹת, דְּכֵיוָן שֶׁנּוֹדַע שֶׁלֹּא גָנְבָה לוֹ, נִמְצָא שֶׁלֹּא הָיָה נֶדֶר:


קונם אשתי נהנית לי שגנבה את כיסו – these are vows made in error, for since it became known that she did not steal from him, it is found that there was no vow.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:2:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:2:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:2:7)


**אֵלּוּ וָאֵלּוּ מֻתָּרִין.** דְּנֶדֶר שֶׁהֻתַּר מִקְצָתוֹ הֻתַּר כֻּלּוֹ, דְּאֵינוֹ רוֹצֶה שֶׁיָּחוּל נִדְרוֹ אֶלָּא כְּעֵין שֶׁנָּדַר אוֹתוֹ, וְכֵיוָן שֶׁמִּקְצָתוֹ הָיָה שׁוֹגֵג נִתְבַּטֵּל כֻּלּוֹ:


אלו ואלו מותרין – for a vow that was partially permitted is completely permitted, for he does not want that his vow would take place, but rather, similar to that he made the vow, and since part of it was in made inadvertently in error, all of it is nullified.



### Mishnah 3

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:3:1)


**נִדְרֵי אֳנָסִין.** דְּמֵעִקָּרָא לֹא הָיָה בְּדַעְתּוֹ שֶׁיָּחוּל הַנֶּדֶר אִם יְעַכְּבֶנּוּ אֹנֶס, וּבִכְהַאי גַּוְנָא שֶׁהַדְּבָרִים מוֹכִיחִים, דְּבָרִים שֶׁבַּלֵּב הָווּ דְבָרִים:


מדרי אונסין – that from the initial point, it was not his intention that the vow would come to pass if an unavoidable interference would prevent him, and in a case like this when the matters prove it, the matters of the heart are matters.



### Mishnah 4

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:4:1)


**לָהֳרָגִין.** לִסְטִים שֶׁהוֹרְגִין הָאָדָם וְנוֹטְלִין אֶת מָמוֹנוֹ:


להרגין – robbers that kill a person and take his money.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:4:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:4:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:4:2)


**וְלָחֳרָמִין.** גַּזְלָנִים שֶׁאֵינָם הוֹרְגִים. וְלֹא זוֹ אַף זוֹ קָתָנֵי:


ולחרמים – thieves that do not kill, and not only this, even that is taught (i.e., the cases in the Mishnah that are in descending order of demonstrability).


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:4:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:4:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:4:3)


**וְלַמּוֹכְסִין.** בְּמוֹכֵס הָעוֹמֵד מֵאֵלָיו. אֲבָל מוֹכֵס שֶׁהֶעֱמִידוֹ הַמֶּלֶךְ, בֵּין מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל בֵּין מֶלֶךְ נָכְרִי, וְלוֹקֵחַ דָּבָר קָצוּב בְּחֹק הַמַּלְכוּת, דִּינָא דְמַלְכוּתָא דִינָא וְאָסוּר לִבְרֹחַ מִן הַמֶּכֶס, וְכָל שֶׁכֵּן שֶׁאָסוּר לִדֹּר וְלִשָּׁבַע לוֹ לַשֶּׁקֶר:


ולמוכסין – a customs/tax collector that stands on his own. But a tax-collector that was appointed by the king (i.e., “the state”) whether the king is an Israelite or a non-Jew and takes a specified amount as the law of the kingdom, “the law of the land is the law,” and it is forbidden to flee from the tax-collector, and all the more so, that it is forbidden to take a vow or to swear to him a falsehood/lie.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:4:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:4:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:4:4)


**שֶׁהֵן שֶׁל תְּרוּמָה.** אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁהוֹרְגִין וְגוֹזְלִין, אֵין אוֹכְלִין דָּבָר הָאָסוּר. אִי נַמִּי, תְּרוּמָה לֹא חֲשִׁיבָא לְהוּ, מִתּוֹךְ שֶׁאֵינָהּ נֶאֱכֶלֶת אֶלָּא לְכֹהֲנִים טְהוֹרִים נִמְכֶּרֶת בְּזוֹל הַרְבֵּה:


שהן של תרומה – even though they kill and steal, we don’t eat something that is forbidden (other than to Kohanim). Alternatively, heave-offering is not important to them because it is not eaten other to pure Kohanim and is sold very cheaply.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:4:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:4:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:4:5)


**לֹא יִפְתַּח לוֹ בְנֶדֶר.** אִם לֹא שָׁאַל מִמֶּנּוּ הָאַנָּס שֶׁיִּדֹּר לֹא יַתְחִיל הוּא בַּנֶּדֶר:


לא יפתח לו בנדר – if the violent man did not ask of him to take an oath, he should not begin with an oath.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:4:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:4:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:4:6)


**בְּמַה שֶּׁהוּא מַדִּירוֹ.** אִם שָׁאַל מִמֶּנּוּ הָאַנָּס לִדֹּר לֹא יִדֹּר לוֹ אֶלָּא בְּמַה שֶּׁשָּׁאַל בִּלְבַד, וְלֹא יִדֹּר לוֹ בְּדָבָר אַחֵר. וְכָל הָנֵי אַרְבָּעָה נְדָרִים דְּתָנָא בְּמַתְנִיתִין, דִּין הַנְּדָרִים וְהַשְּׁבוּעוֹת שָׁוִים, וּמַה שֶּׁמֻּתָּר בְּנֶדֶר מֻתָּר בִּשְׁבוּעָה וְאֵין צְרִיכִים הַתָּרָה, חוּץ מִנִּדְרֵי זֵרוּזִין בִּלְבַד שֶׁצְּרִיכִים הַתָּרָה מִדִּבְרֵי סוֹפְרִים, לְפִיכָךְ הַשְּׁבוּעָה אֲסוּרָה בָּהֶם:


כמה שהוא מדירו ([one takes a vow] only in the matter concerning which a vow is imposed) – if the violent man did not ash of hm to take a vow, he should not take a vow to him other than in what he asked alone, and not take a vow to him in another matter. And ll of these four vows that are taught in our Mishnah, the laws of vows and oaths are equal, but what is permissible in a vow is permissible in an oath and it doesn’t require a release, except for vows of incitement alone, that require release from the words of the Scribes, therefore, an oath is forbidden with them.



### Mishnah 5

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:5:1)


**הֲרֵי נְטִיעוֹת הָאֵלּוּ קָרְבָּן אִם אֵינָן נִקְצָצוֹת.** רָאָה רוּחַ סְעָרָה בָּאָה וְיָרֵא שֶׁמָּא יִקָּצְצוּ נְטִיעוֹתָיו וְאָמַר הֲרֵי אֵלּוּ קָרְבָּן אִם אֵינָן נִקְצָצוֹת. אוֹ שֶׁרָאָה דְּלֵקָה נָפְלָה בָעִיר וְיָרֵא עַל טַלִּיתוֹ שֶׁמָּא תִּשָּׂרֵף וְאָמַר הֲרֵי זוֹ קָרְבָּן אִם לֹא תִשָּׂרֵף:


הרי נטיעות האלו קרבן אם אינן נקצצות – he saw the storm-wind and was afraid lest it would cut down his plants, and he said, “Lo, these are KORBAN (i.e., dedicated to the Temple) if they are not cut down.” Or he saw conflagration that fell upon the city and was fearful about his cloak lest it would be burned and he states, “Lo this is a KORBAN, if it is not burned.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:5:2)


**יֵשׁ לָהֶם פִּדְיוֹן.** כִּשְׁאָר הֶקְדֵּשׁוֹת, וְיִפָּדוּ וְיִקְנֶה בִדְמֵיהֶן קָרְבָּן. דְּכֵיוָן דְּלֹא אָמַר הֲרֵי הֵן עָלַי כְּקָרְבָּן, לָאו לְמֵסְרִינְהוּ עֲלֵיהּ כְּקָרְבָּן אִכַּוַּן אֶלָּא שֶׁיִּהְיוּ לִקְנוֹת בִּדְמֵיהֶן קָרְבָּן:


יש להם פדיון – like the rest of the things dedicated [to the Temple], and he can redeem them and purchase with their monetary proceeds a sacrifice. But since he didn’t say, “Lo, these are upon me as a KORBAN,” they are not to be delivered as a sacrifice, but rather, that he will be able to purchase with their monetary proceeds a sacrifice.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:5:3)


**אֵין לָהֶם פִּדְיוֹן.** אֶלָּא הַמָּעוֹת נִתְפָּסִין בִּקְדֻשָּׁה וְהַנְּטִיעוֹת חוֹזְרוֹת לִהְיוֹת קְדוֹשׁוֹת, דְּכֵיוָן דְּאָמַר עַד שֶׁיִּקָּצְצוּ, הָכִי קָאָמַר, לִכְשֶׁאֶפְדֵּם יַחְזְרוּ וְיִקְדְּשׁוּ עַד שֶׁיִּקָּצְצוּ:


אין להם פדיון – but rather the money is seized in its holiness and the plants return to become holy, for since he said, “until they are cut down,” this is what he said: when I redeem they will return and be sanctified until they are cut down.



### Mishnah 6

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:6:1)


**מֻתָּר בְּיוֹשְׁבֵי יַבָּשָׁה.** שֶׁאֵין דַּרְכָּם לָרֶדֶת בַּיָּם:


מותר ביושבי יבשה – for it is nt their practice to go down to the sea.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:6:2)


**בִּכְלָל יוֹשְׁבֵי יַבָּשָׁה.** שֶׁסּוֹפָם לָרֶדֶת וְלֵישֵׁב בַּיַּבָּשָׁה:


בכלל יושבי היבשה – for eventually they will go down [to the sea] and to dwell on dry land.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:6:3)


**לֹא כָאֵלּוּ הַהוֹלְכִים מֵעַכּוֹ לְיָפוֹ.** אִית דִּמְפָרְשִׁי לַהּ הָכִי, הָא דִתְנַן בָּרֵישָׁא דְמַתְנִיתִין הַנּוֹדֵר מִיּוֹרְדֵי הַיָּם מֻתָּר בְּיוֹשְׁבֵי יַבָּשָׁה, דְּמַשְׁמַע הָא בְיוֹרְדֵי הַיָּם אָסוּר, לֹא כָאֵלּוּ שֶׁהוֹלְכִים מֵעַכּוֹ לְיָפוֹ, שֶׁהַנּוֹדֵר מִיּוֹרְדֵי הַיָּם אֵינוֹ אָסוּר בָּהֶם, דְּמִשּׁוּם דֶּרֶךְ מֻעָט כָּזֶה לֹא מִקְּרוּ יוֹרְדֵי הַיָּם. וְאִית דִּמְפָרְשִׁי, לֹא כָאֵלּוּ הַהוֹלְכִים מֵעַכּוֹ לְיָפוֹ בִּלְבַד, דְּבִכְלַל יוֹרְדֵי הַיָּם הֵם לֵאָסֵר וְגַם יוֹשְׁבֵי יַבָּשָׁה מִקְּרוּ, אֶלָּא אַף בְּמִי שֶׁדַּרְכּוֹ לְפָרֵשׁ, לְפִי שֶׁסּוֹפוֹ לֵירֵד לַיַּבָּשָׁה:


לא כאלו ההולכים מעכו ליפו – there are those who interpret this as such: That which is taught in the opening clause of our Mishnah: “He who takes a vow [not to gain benefit] from those who go down to the sea, are permitted [to gain benefit] from those who dwell on dry land, which implies, that those who go down to the sea are prohibited [to gain benefit from them], not the like of those who go from Acre to Jaffa, for a person who makes a vow from those who go down to the sea is not prohibited to them, for because of a short distance like this, they are not called, “those who go down to the sea.” There are those interpret: “not like those who go from Acre to Jaffa lon, because hey are included in those who go down to the sea that are prohibited, but also they are called hose who dwell on dry land, but rather, even those sail out of sight, because eventually, they will go down to dry land.



### Mishnah 7

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:7:1)


**מִי שֶׁהַחַמָּה רוֹאָה אוֹתוֹ.** מִדְּלֹא קָאָמַר מִן הָרוֹאִים:


מי שהחמה רואה אותו – for he didn’t say from those who see [the sun].



### Mishnah 8

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:8:1)


**אָסוּר בַּקֵּרְחִים וּבְבַעֲלֵי הַשֵּׂיבוֹת.** מִדְּלֹא קָאָמַר בְּבַעֲלֵי הַשֵּׂעָר:


אמר בקרחים ובבעלי השיבות – since he didn’t say, “with those who have hair.”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:8:2)


**שֶׁאֵין נִקְרָאִים שְׁחוֹרֵי הָרֹאשׁ אֶלָּא אֲנָשִׁים.** לְפִי שֶׁהָאֲנָשִׁים פְּעָמִים מְכַסִּים רֹאשָׁם וּפְעָמִים מְגַלִּים וּמִשַּׁחֲרוּת רֹאשָׁם נִכָּר שֶׁהֵם אֲנָשִׁים, אֲבָל נָשִׁים לְעוֹלָם הוֹלְכוֹת וְרֹאשָׁם מְכֻסֶּה. וְהַקְּטַנִּים בֵּין זְכָרִים בֵּין נְקֵבוֹת הוֹלְכִין בְּגִלּוּי הָרֹאשׁ וְאֵינָם נִכָּרִים בֵּין זָכָר לִנְקֵבָה, וּמִשּׁוּם הָכִי לֹא אִקְּרוּ שְׁחוֹרֵי הָרֹאשׁ אֶלָּא הָאֲנָשִׁים הַגְּדוֹלִים:


שאין נקראים שחורי הראש אלא אנשים – because the men sometimes cover heir heads and sometimes reveal and blacken their heads that is recognized that they are men, but women always walk and their heads are covered. But the small children, whether boys or girls walk with their heads revealed and they are not recognized as to whether they are boys or girls, and because of this, they are not called, “black-haired,” but rather, the adult men.



### Mishnah 9

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:9:1)


**מִן הַיִּלּוֹדִים.** מַשְׁמַע שֶׁנּוֹלְדוּ כְבָר:


מן שילודים – implying that they have already been born.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:9:2)


**מִן הַנּוֹלָדִים.** מַשְׁמַע הָעֲתִידִין לְהִוָּלֵד:


מן הנולדים – implying those who will in the future be born.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:9:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:9:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:9:3)


**מִן הַנּוֹלָדִים רַבִּי מֵאִיר מַתִּיר אַף בַּיִּלּוֹדִים.** בַּגְּמָרָא מְפָרֵשׁ דְּחַסּוֹרֵי מִחַסְּרָא וְהָכִי קָתָנֵי, מִן הַנּוֹלָדִים אָסוּר בַּיִּלּוֹדִים, רַבִּי מֵאִיר אוֹמֵר אַף הַנּוֹדֵר מִן הַנּוֹלָדִים מֻתָּר בַּיִּלּוֹדִים, כִּי הֵיכִי דְּהַנּוֹדֵר מִן הַיִּלּוֹדִים מֻתָּר בַּנּוֹלָדִים:


מן הנולדים רבי מאיר מתיר אף בילודים (from those who are born) – In the Gemara (Tractate Nedarim 30b) it explains that the Mishnah is deficient and should be read as follows: [those who take a vow] from those who may be born thereafter, are prohibited to those who are already born. Rabbi Meier states, even those who make a vow [to be prohibited] from those who will be born thereafter are permitted to those who are [already] born, in order that one who takes a vow [to be prohibited from those who have been already been born are permitted to those who may be born thereafter.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:9:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:9:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:9:4)


**אֶלָּא מִמִּי שֶׁדַּרְכּוֹ לְהוֹלִיד.** כְּגוֹן אָדָם וּבְהֵמָה, לַאֲפוֹקֵי עוֹפוֹת וְדָגִים שֶׁאֵין מוֹלִידִים אֶלָּא מְטִילִים בֵּיצִים:


אלא ממי שדרכו להוליד – as for example, humans and beasts, to exclude fowl and fish who do not give birth but rather lay eggs.



### Mishnah 10

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:10:1)


**מֵאוֹכְלֵי שׁוּם.** אֶחָד מֵעֶשֶׂר תַּקָּנוֹת שֶׁתִּקֵּן עֶזְרָא שֶׁיִּהְיוּ אוֹכְלִים שׁוּם לֵילֵי שַׁבָּת, מִפְּנֵי שֶׁמַּרְבִּים הַזֶּרַע וְלֵיל שַׁבָּת הוּא זְמַן עוֹנָה שֶׁל תַּלְמִידֵי חֲכָמִים:


מאוכלי שום – one of ten enactments made by Ezra [the Scribe] that they would eat garlic on Friday evenings, because it increases seed (i.e., semen) and Friday evening is the time for fulfillment of marital duties of Sages (see also Tractate Ketubot, Chapter 5, Mishnah 6).


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:10:2)


**וּמֻתָּר בַּכּוּתִים.** שֶׁאֵינָן עוֹלִים לָרֶגֶל, וְאַף עַל גַּב דְּמִדְּאוֹרַיְתָא הוּא, מִפְּנֵי שֶׁהֵם שׂוֹנְאִים יְרוּשָׁלַיִם וּבָחֲרוּ לָהֶם הַר גְּרִיזִים:


ומותר בכותים – they do not make pilgrimage [to Jerusalem]. But even though that this is according to the Torah, because they hare Jerusalem and chose for themselves Mount Gerizim.



### Mishnah 11

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:1)


**מֻתָּר בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל.** שֶׁיָּצְאוּ מִכְּלַל בְּנֵי נֹחַ:


מותר בישראל – they have department from the category of the children of Noah/Noahides.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:2)


**וְאָסוּר בְּעוֹבְדֵי כוֹכָבִים.** וַאֲפִלּוּ בְּאוֹתָם שֶׁהֵם מִזֶּרַע אַבְרָהָם:


ואסור באומות העולם – and even those who are from the seed of Abraham (i.e. Abraham’s other children such as Ishmael or the children of Keturah, etc.).


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:11:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:3)


**אָסוּר בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל.** וְגֵרִים נַמִּי בִּכְלַל זֶרַע אַבְרָהָם נִינְהוּ, דִּכְתִיב (בראשית יז) כִּי אַב הֲמוֹן גּוֹיִם נְתַתִּיךָ:


אסור בישראל – and converts also are within the category of he seed of Abraham, as it is written (Genesis 17:5): “[but your name shall be Abraham,] for I make you the father of a multitude of nations.”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:11:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:4)


**וּמֻתָּר בְּעוֹבְדֵי כוֹכָבִים.** וַאֲפִלּוּ בְאוֹתָם שֶׁהֵם מִזֶּרַע אַבְרָהָם, דְּלֹא אִקְּרוּ זֶרַע אַבְרָהָם אֶלָּא בְּנֵי יַעֲקֹב בִּלְבַד, דִּכְתִיב (שם כא) כִּי בְיִצְחָק יִקָּרֵא לְךָ זָרַע, בְּיִצְחָק וְלֹא כָּל יִצְחָק:


ומותר באומות העולם – and even with those who are from the seed of Abraham, for only the children of Jacob were called the seed of Abraha, as it is written (Genesis 21:12): “for it is through Isaac that offspring shall be continued/called for you,” but no all of Isaac/כל יצחק (i.e., Esau).


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:11:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:5)


**שֶׁאֵינִי נֶהֱנֶה לְיִשְׂרָאֵל.** מִשֶּׁל יִשְׂרָאֵל:


שאיני נהנה לישראל – that of Israel.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:11:6

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:6](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:6)


**שֶׁאֵין יִשְׂרָאֵל נֶהֱנִים לִי.** מִשֶּׁלִּי:


שאין ישראל נהנין לי – of what is mine.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:11:7

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:7](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:7)


**וּמוֹכֵר בְּיוֹתֵר, אִם שׁוֹמְעִין לוֹ.** גָּרְסִינַן. כְּלוֹמַר יִמְכֹּר הַדָּבָר בְּיוֹתֵר מִכְּדֵי שָׁוְיוֹ אִם יִרְצֶה חֲבֵרוֹ לִשְׁמֹעַ לוֹ, וְלִקְנוֹת מִמֶּנּוּ הַחֵפֶץ בְּיוֹתֵר מִמַּה שֶּׁשָּׁוֶה:


ומוכר ביותר. אם שומעין לו – this is the reading, meaning to say, he will sell the thing for more from such as it is of its value, if his fellow wishes to listen to him, and to purchase from him the object for more than what it is worth.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:11:8

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:8](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:8)


**לָעֲרֵלִים.** דַּעְתּוֹ עַל מִי שֶׁאֵינוֹ מַאֲמִין בִּבְרִית מִילָה:


לערלים – the opinion on whomever does not believe in ritual circumcision.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:11:9

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:9](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:9)


**לַמּוּלִים.** הַמַּאֲמִינִים בִּבְרִית מִילָה:


למולים – that believe in ritual circumcision.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:11:10

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:10](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:10)


**מֻתָּר בְּעַרְלֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל.** כְּגוֹן מִי שֶׁמֵּתוּ אֶחָיו מֵחֲמַת מִילָה:


מותר בערלי ישראל – as for example, whomever whose brother died on account of [ritual] circumcision.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:11:11

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:11](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:11)


**וְאָסוּר בְּמוּלֵי אֻמּוֹת.** כְּגוֹן עֲרָבִי מָהוּל וְגִבְעוֹנִי מָהוּל:


ואסור במולי אומות – as for example, an Arab who is [ritually] circumcised or a Gibeonite who is [ritually] circumcised.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:11:12

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:12](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:12)


**שְׁלֹשׁ עֶשְׂרֵה בְרִיתוֹת.** נֶאֶמְרוּ בְּפָרָשַׁת מִילָה שֶׁנֶּאֶמְרָה לְאַבְרָהָם:


שלש עשרה בריתות – it is stated in the portion of circumcision (Chapter 17 of Genesis, where the word "ברית"/covenant, is mentioned thirteen times) which is mentioned to Abraham.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:11:13

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:13](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:13)


**שֶׁהִיא דּוֹחָה אֶת הַשַּׁבָּת.** דִּכְתִיב (ויקרא יב) וּבַיּוֹם הַשְּׁמִינִי יִמּוֹל בְּשַׂר עָרְלָתוֹ, וַאֲפִלּוּ בְּשַׁבָּת:


שהיא דוחה את השבת – as I is written (Leviticus 12:3): “On the eighth day the flesh of his foreskin shall be circumcised,” and even if [the eighth day is] on Shabbat.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 3:11:14

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:14](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/3:11:14)


**שֶׁהִיא דּוֹחָה אֶת הַנְּגָעִים.** דְּדָרְשִׁינַן יִמּוֹל, וַאֲפִלּוּ בִּמְקוֹם בַּהֶרֶת, וְאֵין כָּאן מִשּׁוּם קוֹצֵץ בַּהַרְתּוֹ שֶׁהוּא בְּלָאו דְּהִשָּׁמֶר בְּנֶגַע הַצָּרַעַת (דברים כד):


שהיא דוחה את הנגעים – that we expound, "ימול"/shall be circumcised (Leviticus 12:3), and even in the place of a white spot on the skin (eventually one of the symptoms of leprosy), but we don’t have here because of cutting off his white spot, which is part of the negative commandment of (Deuteronomy 24:8): “In cases of a skin affection [be most careful to do do exactly as the Levitical priests instruct you].”



## Chapter 4


### Mishnah 1

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 4:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/4:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/4:1:1)


**אֵין בֵּין הַמֻּדָּר.** אֶלָּא דְרִיסַת הָרֶגֶל. לַעֲבֹר דֶּרֶךְ אַרְצוֹ, אוֹ לְהַשְׁאִיל כֵּלִים שֶׁאֵין עוֹשִׂין בָּהֶם אֹכֶל נֶפֶשׁ, שֶׁמֻּתָּרִין בַּמֻּדָּר הֵימֶנּוּ מַאֲכָל, וַאֲסוּרִין בַּמֻּדָּר הֵימֶנּוּ הֲנָאָה:


אין בין מודר. אלא דריסת הרגל – to pass through his land, or to lend out utensils that they don’t make in them food necessary for the Sabbath day, which are permissible for someone who took a vow of deriving food from him, but is forbidden if he took a vow to enjoying any benefit from him.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 4:1:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/4:1:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/4:1:2)


**נָפָה וּכְבָרָה רֵחַיִם וְתַנּוּר.** שֶׁמְּתַקְּנִים בָּהֶם אֹכֶל נֶפֶשׁ. וְכָל שֶׁכֵּן קְדֵרָה וְשַׁפּוּד שֶׁהָאֹכֶל עוֹמֵד בְּתוֹכוֹ. וּמֻדָּר מַאֲכָל דִּתְנַן בְּמַתְנִיתִין, הוּא שֶׁיֹּאמַר לוֹ הֲנָאַת מַאֲכָלְךָ עָלַי קוֹנָם:


נפה וכברה רחים ותנור (winnow, basket used as a sieve, millstone, an oven) – that they prepare in them food necessary for the [Sabbath] day. And all the more so, a pot or a spit where the food stands within it. One who takes a vow against food that is taught in our Mishnah, is he who says to him: “KONAM: benefitting from your food is upon me.”


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 4:1:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/4:1:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/4:1:3)


**מְקוֹם שֶׁמַּשְׂכִּירִין כַּיּוֹצֵא בָהֶם.** דְּכֵיוָן דְּדֶרֶךְ לְהַשְׂכִּיר כַּיּוֹצֵא בָּזֶה וְהוּא מוֹחֵל לוֹ הַשְּׂכִירוּת, בְּאוֹתָם הַדָּמִים יָכוֹל לִקְנוֹת אֹכֶל נֶפֶשׁ, וְהוּא הִדִּירוֹ מֵהֲנָאָה הַמְּבִיאָה לִידֵי מַאֲכָל:


מקום שמשכירין כיוצא בהם (a place where these things are rented out) – for since it is the practice to rent out such things and he foregoes the rental payment, with those monies he can purchase food necessary for the [Sabbath] day and he made him take a vow from deriving benefit is brought through the food.



### Mishnah 2

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 4:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/4:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/4:2:1)


**שׁוֹקֵל לוֹ שִׁקְלוֹ.** מַחֲצִית הַשֶּׁקֶל שֶׁחַיָּב כָּל אֶחָד מִיִּשְׂרָאֵל בְּכָל שָׁנָה לְצֹרֶךְ קָרְבְּנוֹת צִבּוּר. וְהַמַּדִּיר יָכוֹל לִשְׁקֹל בִּשְׁבִילוֹ, דְּמִצְוָה בְּעָלְמָא קָעָבֵיד:


שוקל לו שקלו – the half-shekel that everyone from Israel is obligated for in each yer for the needs of the community sacrifices. But the person making he vow is able to pay his portion [for the one-half shekel] for him, for he is performing a mere Mitzvah.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 4:2:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/4:2:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/4:2:2)


**וּפוֹרֵעַ לוֹ חוֹבוֹ.** אִית דְּמוֹקְמִי לַהּ דַּוְקָא בְּחוֹב שֶׁהִתְנָה לֹוֶה עִם הַמַּלְוֶה שֶׁלֹּא יִפְרְעֶנּוּ אֶלָּא לִכְשֶׁיִּרְצֶה וְלֹא יוּכַל הַמַּלְוֶה לְלָחְצוֹ, וְהַשְׁתָּא לֹא אַהֲנֵי לֵיהּ מִידֵי בְּמַה שֶּׁפּוֹרֵעַ חוֹבוֹ. וְאִית דְּמוֹקְמִי לַהּ בְּכָל חוֹב שֶׁבָּעוֹלָם, שֶׁאֵינוֹ אֶלָּא מוֹנֵעַ אֶת בַּעַל חוֹבוֹ שֶׁלֹּא יִתְבְּעֶנּוּ, וּמְנִיעַת תְּבִיעָה אֵינָהּ בִּכְלַל הֲנָאָה:


ופורע לו חובו – here are those who establish it especially regarding a debt that the borrower made a condition/stipulation with the lender that he should not pay back the debt other than when he wanted and the creditor is not able to pressure him, but now he doesn’t benefit him at all when he pays back his debt. But there are those who establish it for every debt that is in the world, which is not other than preventing the creditor that he should not make a claim against him, for the preventing of a claim is not within the category of benefit.


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 4:2:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/4:2:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/4:2:3)


**וּמַחֲזִיר לוֹ אֲבֵדָתוֹ.** בֵּין שֶׁהָיוּ נִכְסֵי מַחֲזִיר אֲסוּרִים עַל בַּעַל אֲבֵדָה, בֵּין שֶׁהָיוּ נִכְסֵי בַּעַל אֲבֵדָה אֲסוּרִים עַל הַמַּחֲזִיר, מִשּׁוּם דְּמִצְוָה קָעָבֵיד:


ומחזיר לו אבדתו – whether the property of the one returning [the object] are forbidden on the person who lost [the object] or whether the property of the person who lost [the object] are forbidden on the person who returns [the object], because he is performing a Mitzvah [of restoring/returning a lost object].


###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 4:2:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/4:2:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/4:2:4)


**וּבִמְקוֹם שֶׁנּוֹטְלִין עָלֶיהָ שָׂכָר תִּפֹּל הֲנָאָה לַהֶקְדֵּשׁ.** כְּשֶׁשְּׁנֵיהֶם מֻדָּרִים הֲנָאָה זֶה מִזֶּה, אִם הוּא נוֹטֵל שָׂכָר נִמְצָא נֶהֱנֶה, וְאִם אֵינוֹ נוֹטֵל שָׂכָר נִמְצָא מְהַנֶּה, לְפִיכָךְ תִּפֹּל הֲנָאָה לַהֶקְדֵּשׁ. וְלֹא אָמְרִינַן יוֹלִיךְ הֲנָאָה לְיַם הַמֶּלַח, לְפִי שֶׁאָסַר עָלָיו הֲנָאָתוֹ כְּהֶקְדֵּשׁ, הִלְכָּךְ כָּל הֲנָאָה הַבָּאָה לְיָדוֹ מִמֶּנּוּ הֶקְדֵּשׁ הִיא:


ובמקום שנוטלין עליה שכר תפול הנאה להקדש – when both of them take a vow of deriving any benefit from each other, if he akes the reward, it is found that he benefits, and if he doesn’t take the reward, it is found that he provides benefit [to the other], therefore, that benefit should fall to the Temple property. But we don’t say that he should bring the benefit to the Dead Sea (literally, Salt Sea), because his benefit is forbidden upon him like something given to the Temple property, therefore, all benefit that comes to his hand from him is Temple property/dedicated to a sacred purpose.



### Mishnah 3

###### Bartenura on Mishnah Nedarim 4:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/4:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Bartenura%20on%20Mishnah%20Nedarim/r/4:3:1)


**וְתוֹרֵם תְּרוּמָתוֹ וּמַעַשְׂרוֹתָיו לְדַעְתּוֹ.** כְּגוֹן שֶׁאָמַר כָּל הָרוֹצֶה לִתְרֹם יָבֹא וְיִתְרֹם. אֲבָל לֹא יֹאמַר לוֹ לִתְרֹם, שֶׁהֲרֵי עוֹשֵׂהוּ שָׁלִיחַ וַהֲנָאָה הִיא לוֹ שֶׁעוֹשֶׂה שְׁלִיחוּתוֹ:


ותורם תרומתו ומעשרותיו לדעתו – as, for example, that he said, all who wish to make a heave-offering should come and make the heave-offering/Terumah. But he should not tell him to make the heave-offering, for behold that makes him an agent and it is a benefit to him that he performs his agency.
